\link_{\sigma}(\nu,\mu)=\link_{\phi}^{f}(\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{N}^{*}\nu),\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu))+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\mathcal{P}}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{k}}}d(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu)\otimes\mu

Lemma 90
.

The map
\link_{\sigma}
is continuous on
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)\times\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
.

Proof.
The maps
\nu\mapsto\mathcal{N}^{*}\mu
and
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}
are continuous (see Proposition
73
). The signed measure
\mathcal{N}^{*}\nu
is supported by a subset of
\prim
(see Lemma
76
), which is finite. Denote by
\Delta\subset M
the union of the periodic orbits containing
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}(\prim)
. The map
\link_{\phi}^{f}
is continuous on the set of pairs of signed measures
(\nu_{\phi},\mu_{\phi})\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)\times\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
for which
\Delta
contains the support of
\nu_{\phi}
(see Proposition
64
). Therefore the term
\link_{\phi}^{f}(\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{N}^{*}\nu),\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu))
is continuous. The second term is continuous according to Proposition
81
.
∎

Lemma 91
.

For any
\nu,\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
with finite supports, so that
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)
and
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu)
have disjoint support, we have

\link_{\sigma}(\nu,\mu)=\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\nu,\mu)

Proof.
Suppose first that the support of
\mu
is also disjoint from
\prim
. Then for all
k
, the support of
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu)
is disjoint from the support of
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)
. Hence according to Theorem
80
, we have
\int_{\Sigma_{\mathcal{P}}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{k}}}d(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu)\otimes\mu=\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu-\mathcal{N}_{{k+1}}^{*}\nu,\mu)
. It follows that:

\displaystyle\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\nu,\mu)

\displaystyle=\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\mathcal{N}^{*}\nu,\mu)+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu-\mathcal{N}_{{k+1}}^{*}\nu,\mu)

\displaystyle=\link_{\phi}^{f}(\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{N}^{*}\nu),\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu))+\sum_{k=1}^{p-1}\int_{\Sigma_{\mathcal{P}}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{k}}}d(\mathcal{N}_{{k}}^{*}\nu)\otimes\mu

\displaystyle=\link_{\sigma}(\nu,\mu)

The general case follows from the density of signed measures in the first case (see Lemma
78
) and from the continuity of
\link_{\sigma}
.
∎

Lemma 92
.

There exists a continuous bilinear map
\link_{\phi}\colon\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)\times\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)\to\mathbb{R}
such that for all signed measures
\nu,\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
, one has
\link_{\sigma}(\nu,\mu)=\link_{\phi}(\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}\nu,\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}\mu)
. Additionally
\link_{\phi}
is continuous on
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)\times\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
.

Proof.
It is enough to prove that for any signed measures
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
with
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{1})=\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{2})
, we have
\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{1},\mu)=\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{2},\mu)
. Once it is proven, we define the map
\link_{\phi}
by
\link_{\phi}(\nu_{\phi},\mu_{\phi})=\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma})
for any signed measures
\nu_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma}\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
in the preimages of
\nu_{\phi},\mu_{\phi}
by the surjection
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{s}(\sigma)\to\mathcal{M}_{s}(\phi)
.

To prove the claim, take
\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
with
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{1})=\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{2})
. Denote by
\Gamma
the set of periodic orbits of
\phi
, contained in the stable/unstable boundary of
\mathcal{P}
. The map
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}
is injective outside the preimage of the set
\cup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(\mathcal{F}^{s}(\gamma)\cup\mathcal{F}^{u}(\gamma))
for the map
\pi_{\mathcal{P}}
. Hence the signed measures
\nu_{i}
coincide on this set.

According to Lemma
69
, the signed measure
\Theta(\nu_{i})
charges the set
\pi^{-1}(\cup_{\gamma\in\Gamma}(\mathcal{F}^{s}(\gamma)\cup\mathcal{F}^{u}(\gamma)))
only on
\Gamma
.
It follows that
\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}
has a finite support. Suppose that
\mu
is finitely supported, and that the support of
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)
is disjoint from the support of
\Theta(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})
. According to Lemma
91
, we have
\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2},\mu)=\link_{\sigma}^{ff}(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2},\mu)=\link_{\phi}^{f}(\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2}),\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu))=0
since
\Theta_{\mathcal{P}}(\nu_{1}-\nu_{2})=0
. By continuity of
\link_{\sigma}
and density of the signed measures satisfying that assumption, we have
\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{1},\mu)=\link_{\sigma}(\nu_{2},\mu)
for all signed measures
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\sigma)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
15
.
Lemma
78
implies that we can approximate any pair of invariant signed measures
(\nu,\mu)\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
by sequence of pairs
(\nu_{n},\mu_{n})\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
, where
\nu_{n}
and
\mu_{n}
are supported by disjoint and finite unions of periodic orbits. Therefore the uniqueness and symmetry of the map
\link_{\phi}
follow from the continuity.

When the stable and unstable foliations of
\phi
are orientable, the theorem follows from the above discussion (see Lemmas
91
and
92
).

Suppose that the stable and unstable foliations are not orientable. We denote by
\widehat{\pi}\colon\widehat{M}\to M
the bundle-orientations covering (which is of degree 2), and by
g
the non-trivial deck transformation of
\widehat{M}\to\widehat{M}
. Lift
\phi
to a flow
\psi
on
\widehat{M}
.
We denote by
\link_{\psi}\colon\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\psi)^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
the linking number map obtained in the orientable case.
Take a signed measure
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
and denote by
\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}\in\mathcal{M}_{s}(\psi)
the signed measure which coincides with
((\widehat{\pi}_{|U})^{-1})^{*}\mu
on each set
U\subset\widehat{M}
where
\widehat{\pi}
is invertible. Notice that
\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}
is the unique
\psi
and
g
invariant signed measure lifting
2\mu
.

Take a closed 1-form
\widehat{\alpha}
on
\widehat{M}
. Since
\widehat{\alpha}+g^{*}\widehat{\alpha}
is invariant by the action of
g
, there exists a closed 1-form
\alpha
on
M
satisfying
\widehat{\pi}^{*}\alpha=\widehat{\alpha}+g^{*}\widehat{\alpha}
. The flow
\psi_{t}
and the signed measure
\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}
are invariant by
g
, so we have

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{M}}\iota\left(\frac{\partial\psi_{t}}{\partial t}\right)\widehat{\alpha}d\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\int_{\widehat{M}}\iota\left(\frac{\partial\psi_{t}}{\partial t}\right)\widehat{\alpha}d\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}+\int_{\widehat{M}}\iota\left(\frac{\partial\psi_{t}}{\partial t}\right)g^{*}\widehat{\alpha}d\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\widehat{M}}\iota\left(\frac{\partial\psi_{t}}{\partial t}\right)\widehat{\pi}^{*}\alpha d\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\iota\left(\frac{\partial\phi_{t}}{\partial t}\right)\alpha d\mu=0

It follows that
\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}
is null-homologous. Take
K\subset\widehat{M}
a measurable subset such that
(K,g\cdot K)
is a partition of
\widehat{M}
. Then the projection
K\to M
is bijective. Given a continuous map
f\colon\widehat{M}\to\mathbb{R}
, one has

\displaystyle\int_{\widehat{M}}fd\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}

\displaystyle=\int_{K}fd\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}+\int_{g\cdot K}fd\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}f\circ(\widehat{\pi}_{K})^{-1}d\mu+\int_{M}f\circ(g\cdot\widehat{\pi}_{K})^{-1}d\mu

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\Bigg(\sum_{y\in\widehat{\pi}^{-1}(x)}f(x)\Bigg)d\mu(x)

It follows that
\mu\mapsto\mu\circ\widehat{\pi}
is continuous.
For
\nu,\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\psi)
, we define
\link_{\phi}(\nu,\mu)=\frac{1}{2}\link_{\psi}(\mu\circ\widehat{\pi},\nu\circ\widehat{\pi})
. By construction, it is continuous.
Take
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
two algebraic multi-orbits for
\phi
. Take
S_{1}
a 2-chain on
M
, bounding
\Gamma_{1}
. Lift
\Gamma_{i}

to an algebraic multi-orbit
\widehat{\Gamma}_{i}
of
\psi
, which satisfies
g\cdot\widehat{\Gamma}_{i}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{i}
. Also lift
S_{1}
to a 2-chain
\widehat{S}_{1}
in
\widehat{M}
, bounding
\widehat{\Gamma}_{1}
. According to the orientable case, we have

\displaystyle\link_{\phi}(\Leb_{\Gamma_{1}},\Leb_{\Gamma_{2}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\link_{\psi}(\Leb_{\Gamma_{1}}\circ\widehat{\pi},\Leb_{\Gamma_{2}}\circ\widehat{\pi})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\link_{\psi}(\Leb_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{1}},\Leb_{\widehat{\Gamma}_{2}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\widehat{S}_{1}\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}\widehat{\Gamma}_{2}

\displaystyle=S_{1}\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}\Gamma_{2}

\displaystyle=\link_{\phi}^{ff}(\Leb_{\Gamma_{1}},\Leb_{\Gamma_{2}})

∎

We need to prove a last lemma.

Proof of Lemma
19
.
Let
\alpha
and
V
be respectively smooth 1-form and 3-form, so that
V
is invariant by
\phi
and
d\alpha=\iota_{X}V
. Denote by
\nu_{V}\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{s}(\phi)
the signed measure induced by
V
. We prove that
\link_{\phi}(\nu_{V},\mu)=\int_{M}\iota_{X}\alpha d\mu
for any
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{p}(\phi)
.

It is enough to verify the equality for signed measures of the form
\Leb_{\Gamma}
for null-homologous algebraic multi-orbits
\Gamma
. The lemma follows from continuity of
\link_{\phi}
and from the density
Lemma
14
.
Denote by
\Delta
the support of
\Gamma
and take a rational 2-chain
S
in
M_{\Gamma}
such that
(\pi_{\Delta})_{*}(\partial S)=\Gamma
. We can take a closed 1-form
\omega
on
M_{\Gamma}
so that
P_{\Delta}([\omega])=[S]
.
From the definition
\link_{\Gamma}^{\Delta}
follows

\displaystyle\link_{\phi}^{f}(\nu_{\alpha},\Leb_{\Gamma})

\displaystyle=\int_{M_{\Delta}}(\iota_{X_{\Delta}}\omega)\pi_{\Delta}^{*}V=\int_{M_{\Delta}}\omega\wedge(\iota_{X_{\Delta}}\pi_{\Delta}^{*}V)

\displaystyle=\int_{M_{\Delta}}\omega\wedge d\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\alpha

\displaystyle=\int_{S}d\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\alpha\qquad\text{by Poincaré duality of $[\omega]$ and $[S]$}

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial S}\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\alpha=\int_{\Gamma}\alpha

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\iota_{X}\alpha d\Leb_{\Gamma}

∎

Appendix A
Birkhoff section with given boundary

We prove
Theorem
H
,
which states that given
\Gamma
, there exists a Birkhoff section bounding
N\Gamma
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
, if and only if
\Leb_{\Gamma}
is Reeb-like. As a corollary, we obtain a second proof of Barbot
Theorem
6
.

Given an oriented compact 3-manifold
N
and a flow
\psi
on
N
, a
global section
of
\psi
is an oriented compact surface
S\subset N
which is positively transverse to the flow, with
\partial S\subset\partial N
and which intersects all orbits of
\psi
. It follows from compactness that
S
intersects every orbit arc of length
T
for some
T>0
.

We start by constructing a Birkhoff section from a global section on a blown-up manifold. Take
\Delta\subset M
a finite union of periodic orbits. Denote by
M_{\Delta}
the blown-up manifold of
M
along
\Delta
, by
\pi_{\Delta}\colon M_{\Delta}\to M
the blow-down projection, and by
\phi^{\Delta}
the lifted flow on
M_{\Delta}
.

Lemma 93
.

Let
S\subset M_{\Delta}
a smooth global section of
\phi^{\Delta}
. Then there exists a smooth function
T\colon S\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying that the image
\pi_{\Delta}\circ\phi_{T}(S)
is a Birkhoff section of
\phi
.

Proof.
In this proof, all isotopies are isotopies along the flow.
There are two types of boundary components for
S
. The curves in
\partial S\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
, for some
\gamma\subset\Delta
, which are homologous to a fiber of
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}\to\gamma
, and the others. We call these boundary components of type 1 and type 2.
We need to prove that up to an isotopy on
S
, the projection
\pi_{\Delta}
restricts to a nice map on
S
. Here ’nice map’ means that for each boundary component
\delta\subset\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
of type 1,
\delta
is sent to a single point in
\gamma
and the image of
S
is smooth close to that point. And for each boundary component
\delta
of type 2,
\pi_{\Delta}
restricts to an immersion close to
\delta
. Then the image of
S
is a Birkhoff section.

Take a boundary component
\delta\subset\partial S\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
of type 1. Then
\delta
is isotopic to one fiber
(see
[
Fri82
, Theorem C]
).
In fact there exists a global isotopy of
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
sending
\partial S\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
to a finite union of fibers of
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}\to\gamma
. We can extend the global isotopy on
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
to a global isotopy on
M_{\Delta}
. Denote by
S_{1}
the image of the surface
S
by the global isotopy along the flow. We can take the global isotopy so that
\pi_{\Delta}(S_{1})
is an embedded and smooth surface, close to the orbits of type 1.

Take a boundary component
\delta\subset\partial S_{1}\cap\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
of type 2. Then similarly
\delta
is isotopic to a smooth curve
\delta^{\prime}
in
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
, transverse to the flow, and transverse to the foliation on
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}
by fibers of
\mathbb{T}_{\gamma}\to\gamma
. Similarly there exists a global isotopy on
M_{\Delta}
, whose image of
S_{1}
is a surface
S_{2}
, and which satisfies that
\pi_{\Delta}(S_{2})
is immersed close to the orbits of type 2. Then
\pi_{\Delta}(S_{2})
is a Birkhoff section.
∎

Proof of Theorem
H
.
The implication
1\Rightarrow 2
is already proven in Section
3
(see Lemma
31
). We prove
2\Rightarrow 1
.

Let
\Gamma
be an algebraic multi-orbit with integer coefficients, so that
\Leb_{\Gamma}
is Reeb-like. Denote by
\Delta
the support of
\Gamma
.
Fix an element
\omega_{1}\in H^{1}(M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})
which satisfies
(\pi_{\Delta})_{*}(\partial P_{\Delta}(\omega_{1}))=[\Gamma]
. Since
\Gamma
has integer coefficients, we can choose
\omega_{1}\in H^{1}(M_{\Delta},\mathbb{Q})
with rational coefficients.
Define the set

F=\{({[{\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\mu}]_{\phi}},{[{\mu}]_{\phi^{\Delta}}}\cdot\omega_{1})\in H_{1}(M,\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{R},\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{p}(\phi^{\Delta})\}

By assumption, we have
{[{\mu}]_{\phi^{\Delta}}}\cdot\omega_{1}=\link_{\phi}(\Leb_{\Gamma},\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\mu)>0
for all probability measure
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{p}(\phi^{\Delta})
. Therefore the intersection
F\cap(0\times(-\infty,0])
is empty. According to
Lemma
22
,
there exists
\eta>0
and a linear map
f\colon H_{1}(M,\mathbb{R})\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying that for all
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{p}(\phi^{\Delta})
, we have
{[{\mu}]_{\phi^{\Delta}}}\cdot\omega_{1}\geq f({[{\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\mu}]_{\phi}})+\eta
.

By Poincaré duality,
f
corresponds to an element
\omega_{2}\in H^{1}(M,\mathbb{R})
. Since
\mathcal{M}_{p}(\phi^{\Delta})
is compact, we can perturbate
f
so that
\omega_{2}
has rational coefficients. Take an integer
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
satisfying that
N(\omega_{1}-\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\omega_{2})
have integer coefficients. Then for all probability measures
\mu\in\mathcal{M}_{p}(\phi^{\Delta})
, we have

{[{\mu}]_{\phi^{\Delta}}}\cdot N(\omega_{1}-\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\omega_{2})\geq N\eta>0

It follows from Schwartzman-Fried-Sullivan Theory
(see
[
Fri82
, Theorem D]
),
that
\phi^{\Delta}
admits a global section
S
whose relative homology class
[S]\in H_{2}(M_{\Delta},\partial M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})
is dual to
N(\omega_{1}-\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\omega_{2})
. According to
Lemma
93
,
the global section can be taken so that
\pi_{\Delta}(S)
is a Birkhoff section of
\phi
. Additionally the boundary of
\pi_{\Delta}(S)
is homologous to
(\pi_{\Delta})_{*}(\partial P_{\Delta}(N(\omega_{1}-\pi_{\Delta}^{*}\omega_{2})))=N[\Gamma]
in
H_{1}(\Delta,\mathbb{R})
. Therefore
N\Gamma
bounds a Birkhoff section.
∎

We should discuss a particular case. When there is no null-homologous invariant probability measure,
Sullivan
[
Sul76
]

proved that the flow admits a global section. Since the flow is Anosov, the first-return map for that global section is conjugated to a linear Anosov diffeomorphism on the torus. In that case, every null-homologous algebraic multi-orbit, with integer coefficients, bounds a Birkhoff section
(see
[
Mar21
, Section 4.2]
).
Hence Theorem
H

is a generalization of the suspension case, where we have less control over the multiplicity at the boundary.

Proof of the remaining equivalences in the Table
B
.
Recall the assertions in the table we prove to be equivalent: there exists a positive Birkhoff section (cell 2) if and only if there exists a Reeb-like invariant probability measure (cell 3) if and only if the flow is orbit equivalent to a Reeb-Anosov flow (cell 4).
Here we prove the implications in the first column of the table: cell 4
\implies
cell 3
\iff
cell 2. The implication cell 4
\implies
cell 2 together with the implication cell 2
\implies
cell 1 (the Anosov flow is positively skewed) stated in Theorem
30
yield a second proof to Barbot’s Theorem
6
: Reeb-Anosov are skewed.

Suppose that there exists
\psi
orbit equivalent to
\phi
, which preserves a contact form
\alpha
with
\alpha\wedge d\alpha>0
(cell 4). Then according to
Theorem
E
,
the
\psi
-invariant probability measure induced by
\alpha\wedge d\alpha
has the Reeb-like property. The Reeb-like property is invariant under orbit equivalence, so there exists a
\phi
-invariant probability measure with the Reeb-like property. Namely it is null-homologous and has only positive linking number with null-homologous
\phi
-invariant probability measures (cell 3).

The implication cell 2
\implies
cell 3 follows from the above discussion
(Lemma
31
).

Suppose that there exists a measure
\mu\in\mathcal{M}^{0}_{p}(\phi)
with the Reeb-like property (cell 3). According to
Lemma
14
,
there exists a sequence of null-homologous invariant 1-cycles
\Gamma_{n}
, with rational coefficients, so that
\Leb_{\Gamma_{n}}
converges toward
\mu
. By continuity of the linking number, for large
n
,
\Leb_{\Gamma_{n}}
has positive linking numbers with all measures in
\mathcal{M}^{0}_{p}(\phi)
. It follows from
Theorem
H

that there exists a positive Birkhoff section (cell 2).
∎

Appendix B
Fried-desingularisation

We prove
Proposition
29
:
given a Birkhoff section
S_{1}
and a partial section
S_{2}
, there exists a Birkhoff section homologous to
[S_{1}]+[S_{2}]
in
H_{2}(M,\partial S_{1}\cup\partial S_{2},\mathbb{R})
.
Take
\Delta=\partial S_{1}\cup\partial S_{2}
,
M_{\Delta}
the blown-up manifold and fix a Riemannian metric
m
on
M_{\Delta}
.
We denote by
D
the diameter of
M_{\Delta}
for
m
, and by
\lVert\cdot\rVert
one norm on
H_{2}(M_{\Delta},\partial M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})
. Define the normalization map

N\colon H_{2}(M_{\Delta},\partial M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})\to H_{2}(M_{\Delta},\partial M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})

given by
N(0)=0
and
N(x)=x/\lVert x\rVert
when
x\neq 0
.

For
x\in M_{\Delta}
and
T\geq 0
, denote by
\gamma_{x}^{T}\colon[0,T+1]\to M_{\Delta}
one map given by
\gamma_{x}^{T}(t)=\phi^{\Delta}_{t}(x)
, for
t\in[0,T]
, and
(\gamma_{x}^{T})_{|[T,T+1]}
is smooth path from
\phi_{T}(x)
to
x
, whose length is at most
D
. The choice of the curve
(\gamma_{x}^{T})_{|[T,T+1]}
has no influence on the following notion. The accumulating points of elements of the form
N([\gamma_{x}^{T}])
, for
T\xrightarrow{}+\infty
, are called
asymptotic directions
. When the ambient manifold is closed,
Schwartzman and Fried
[
Fri82
, Theorem D]

proved that a compact surface is relatively homologous to a global section of flow if and only if it intersects algebraically positively every asymptotic direction. Fried’s proof works with minor modification for compact manifolds with boundary (tangent to the flow lines).

Hryniewicz
[
Hry20
]
proved the existence of a Birkhoff section in a given homology class (relatively to a given set of boundary periodic orbits) under the same condition (intersecting positively all asymptotic directions in the blown-up manifold). Note that Hryniewicz’s definition of Birkhoff sections differs from ours in the general case, but they are equivalent for Anosov flows. He requires that a Birkhoff section intersects every orbit ray (in the future and in the past) instead of intersecting every long enough orbit arc. But in the hyperbolic case, he proves that it is equivalence (see
[
Hry20
, Equation 30]
).

Proof of Proposition
29
.
We lift the surface
S_{i}
to a surface
\widehat{S_{i}}
inside
M_{\Delta}
. We write
\widehat{S}=\widehat{S_{1}}\cup\widehat{S_{2}}
. Since
S_{1}
is a Birkhoff section, there exists
T_{0}>0
for which every orbit arc of length
T_{0}
of
\phi^{\Delta}
intersects
\widehat{S_{1}}
. Recall that
S_{i}
is positively transverse to the
\phi
.
Denote by
C_{1}>0
the supremum of the algebraic intersection of
\widehat{S}
and a smooth curve of length
D
, which is finite. Take
C_{2}>0
for which every closed
\mathcal{C}^{1}
curve
\gamma\subset M_{\Delta}
satisfies
\lVert[\gamma]\rVert\leq C_{2}length_{m}(\gamma)
. Also take
C_{3}>0
the maximum of
\Big\lVert\frac{\partial\phi^{\Delta}_{t}}{\partial t}\Big\rVert_{m}
, so that the length for
m
of an orbit arc
\phi_{[0,T]}(x)
is at most
C_{3}T
.

Take
x\in M_{\Delta}
and
T>0
. When
x
is not in the boundary of
M_{\Delta}
, the algebraic intersection between
\gamma_{x}^{T}
and
\widehat{S}
is at least

\phi^{\Delta}_{[0,T]}(x)\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}([\widehat{S_{1}}]+[\widehat{S_{2}}])-C_{1}\geq\frac{T}{T_{0}}-1-C_{1}

By continuity, it also holds for
x\in\partial M_{\Delta}
. So we have

\displaystyle N\big([\gamma_{x}^{T}]\big)\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}\widehat{S}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma_{x}^{T}\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}\widehat{S}}{\big\lVert[\gamma_{x}^{T}]\big\rVert}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\frac{T}{T_{0}}-1-C_{1}}{C_{2}(C_{3}T+D)}

\displaystyle\liminf_{T\xrightarrow{}+\infty}N\big([\gamma_{x}^{T}]\big)\mathrlap{\hskip 2.3pt\cdot}{\cap}\widehat{S}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{T_{0}C_{2}C_{3}}

Hence
\widehat{S}
intersects positively every asymptotic direction. Fried proved in similar settings that there exists a global section relatively homologous to
\widehat{S}
in
H_{2}(M_{\Delta},\partial M_{\Delta},\mathbb{R})
. More precisely when the manifold playing the role of
M_{\Delta}
is closed (Theorem D in Fried’s article) or when the flow is transverse to the boundary of
M_{\Delta}
: in Fried’s Theorem E, global section to the flow restricted to an invariant set are characterized in a similar manner. One can check that Fried’s proof holds for compact manifold with boundary tangent to the flow. Alternatively one can reprove it using a doubling manifold (of
M_{\Delta}
) and applying the closed case. Or one can add a thick torus to all boundary components of
M_{\Delta}
, extend the flow to be transverse to the boundary, and apply Theorem E to the complement of the thick tori.

The existence of a Birkhoff section homologous to
[S_{1}]+[S_{2}]
follows from
Lemma
93
.
∎

Appendix C
Hart lemma for Hölder regularity

We give a proof of the following adaptation of Hart’s lemma. For a flow
\Psi
and a vector field
Z
, we denote by
f^{*}\Psi_{t}=f\circ\Psi_{t}\circ f^{-1}
the flow conjugated to
\Psi
by
f
and by
f^{*}Z=df(Z\circ f^{-1})
. In particular we have
\frac{\partial f^{*}\Psi_{t}}{\partial t}=f^{*}\frac{\partial\Psi_{t}}{\partial t}
.

Lemma 94
(Extended Hart’s lemma
[
Har83
]
).

Let
k\geq 1
and
\Psi
be a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
flow on a smooth compact manifold
M
, and suppose that
\frac{\partial\Psi_{t}}{\partial t}
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of a compact set
K\subset M
. Then there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism
f\colon M\to M
such that
f_{|K}=\id_{K}
and
\frac{\partial f^{*}\Psi_{t}}{\partial t}
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
.

The key argument is, in a local chart, to integrate along the flow to regularize its generating vector field.
Take the space
E=\mathbb{R}^{m}
for some
m>0
and
\Psi
a flow of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
E
. Let
\Psi
be a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
flow on
E
and denote by
Z
the generating vector field of
\Psi
.

Fix three open subsets
U,V,W\subset E
such that
\overline{W}\subset V
,
\overline{V}\subset U
,
\overline{V}\cap\partial U=\emptyset
and
\overline{U}
is compact. Let
T>0
be such that
\Phi_{T}(V)\subset U
, and
c\colon U\to[0,T]
be a
\mathcal{C}^{2+\alpha}
function such that
c
is constant on
W
,
c>0
on
V
and
c\equiv 0
outside
V
. Define the map
F_{c}\colon U\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
by

F_{c}(x)=\int_{0}^{1}\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds

Lemma 95
.

When
\lVert c\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}}
is small enough,
F_{c}
is well-defined on all
U
and satisfies:

•

F_{c}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism on
U
,

•

F_{c}\equiv\id
on
F_{c}(U\setminus V)
,

•

F_{c}^{*}Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
F_{c}(W)
,

•

if
Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
\phi_{[0,T]}(x)
, then
F_{c}^{*}Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
F_{c}(x)
.

Additionally
F_{c}
convergences toward the identity for the
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
topology, when
\lVert c\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}}
goes to zero.

Proof.
The maps
x\mapsto\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)
are
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
-close to the identity map when
\lVert c\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}}
is small. So
\lVert F_{c}-\id\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}}
is converges to zero when
\lVert c\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}}
goes to zero. Since the set of
\mathcal{C}^{1}
embedding is open in the set of
\mathcal{C}^{1}
map,
F_{c}
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism on its image when
T
is small.

Notice that
F_{c}
coincides with the identity map on
U\setminus\overline{V}\supset\partial U
. So for
T
small, we have
F_{c}(U)=U
and
F_{c}\colon U\to U
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism. For
x
in
U
, we have:

\displaystyle dF_{c}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}d\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds+dc(x)\int_{0}^{1}sZ\circ\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds

\displaystyle dF_{c}(x)(Z(x))

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}Z\circ\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds+(dc(x)(Z(x)))\int_{0}^{1}sZ\circ\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c(x)}(\Psi_{c(x)}(x)-x)+(dc(x)(Z(x)))\int_{0}^{1}sZ\circ\Psi_{c(x)s}(x)ds\text{ when }c(x)\neq 0

Since we have
c(x)\neq 0
and
dc\equiv 0
on
W
,
dF_{c}(Z)
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
W
.
When
Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
\Psi_{[0,T]}(x)
,the second member in
dF_{c}(x)(Z(x))
is also
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
x
. So
dF_{c}(Z)
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
x
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
94
.
Let
(A,B_{1},\cdots,B_{n})
be an open covering of
M
such that
K\subset A
,
K\cap\overline{B_{i}}=\emptyset
,
Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
\overline{A}
, and such that there exists
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
embedding from each
\overline{B}_{i}
to
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We prove by induction on
n
that there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphisms
f\colon M\to M
such that
f^{*}Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
M
.

The property is clear for
n=0
. Assume that the property is satisfied for
n>0
. Take
C
an open neighborhood of
\overline{B}_{1}
, disjoint from
K
and
g\colon C\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
embedding with pre-compact image. We take
U=\im(g)
, and
V,W\subset U
such that
\overline{g(B_{1})}\subset W
,
\overline{W}\subset V
and
\overline{V}\subset U
. By assumption, there exists a small
T>0
so that
Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on the set
\Psi_{[0,T](\overline{A})}
. Take a some
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
-small function
c\colon U\to[0,T]
and denote by
F_{c}\colon U\to U
the
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism as in Lemma
95
. According to the same lemma,
(F_{c}\circ g)^{*}Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
W\cup g(A\cap C)
.

Define a map
h\colon M\to M
by
h\equiv\id
on
M\setminus C
and
h\equiv g^{-1}\circ F_{c}\circ g
on
C
.
The map
F_{c}
is equal to the identity on a neighborhood of
\partial U
, so
h
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on all
M
. We can take
F_{c}
arbitrarily
\mathcal{C}^{1}
close to the identity so that
F_{c}(W)
contains
\overline{g(B_{1})}
. Then
h^{*}Z
is of class
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
\overline{B_{1}}
. Similarly we can take
F_{c}
such that
h^{*}Z
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
g(\overline{A}\cap C)
. So
h^{*}Z
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
\overline{A}\cap B_{1}
, and also on
\overline{A}\cap(M\setminus B_{1})
by hypothesis.

The map
h
is a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism such that
h^{*}Z
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on a neighborhood of
\overline{A\cup B_{1}}
. According to the induction hypothesis, there exists a
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
diffeomorphism
i\colon M\to M
such that
i^{*}(h^{*}Z)
is
\mathcal{C}^{k+\alpha}
on
M
, and with
i_{|K}=\id_{K}
. So
f=i\circ h
satisfies the property at the rank
n
, which concludes the induction.
∎
